It cannot be set back to review by hand: it returns to review when what took it down comes back, or by Undo."
77. **D2-77** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): `ItemRecord.redo` gains an optional `kind`: `cascade` (the reducer's Reject, plain and Shaky cascades), `question` (DO-0's question change), `judge` (reconcile's reverts, their dependents and premise re-checks), `take-out` (Submit's, D2-57) and `queue` (RV-3 at a queue's end). Only `cascade` comes back by itself. A later cause keeps a record's kind, except that `question` replaces it (D2-81) and a judge's take-out makes it `judge`. A record without `kind` (saved before 2j1) is read as never coming back by itself, and its cause is shown as a cascade's (its listed causes, else its markers). *Alternative:* read a record without `kind` as a cascade, which would bring back old records of RV-3, DO-0's question and Submit's take-out against D2-76's edge cases (none of them can be told apart from a cascade's by its other fields).
78. **D2-78** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): "nothing else keeps it down" means: no other id in its `because` is declined, to be redone, withdrawn, or outside ws/ (an Add premise back in review), unless it comes back in the same action; no RV-5 dependency of it (the stage judgement's) is declined or to be redone, the same way; and its content is in ws/, or comes back with the request's inverse merges (it was taken out by a declining commit the request un-rejects). The set that comes back is a greatest fixpoint over the candidates, so items that depend on each other come back together. An item taken out by one Reject and kept out by a second, whose first Reject is un-rejected first, is taken out again by that un-reject's write (as before); the second un-reject's merge cannot bring it back, so it stays to be redone, its content out, for Claude to redo at Submit review or Undo to restore (the order the 2e test "it comes back with the decline that took it out" already accepted). *Alternative:* also merge, at that second un-reject, the commit that took the item out first: a change to the merges, which D2-76 keeps as they are.
79. **D2-79** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): an item set back to review releases what is to be redone because of it only when it then holds up: in review with its content in ws/. So an un-rejected Add premise (in review, outside ws/, AF2) and an un-declined item that goes back to be redone (its content a cascade took out before its decline, D2-60) release nothing, and the records that list them are left unchanged. *Alternative:* release on every un-decline, which would bring back to review a Result whose content needs a premise that is not written.
80. **D2-80** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): every cause that takes an item down is recorded in its `because`, so a return reads all of them: a Shaky decline also adds itself to dependents already to be redone (the Reject cascade did already), the judge's take-out adds the reverted item to dependents it had already taken out, and a candidate that a dependency keeps down gains that dependency. *Alternative:* record only the cause that first took an item down; then a Reject followed by a Shaky decline of another item it depends on would let the Reject's un-reject bring it back while the Shaky decline still holds it.
81. **D2-81** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): DO-0's question change sets the Result's `kind` to `question` also when the plain decline's cascade has just taken it (its proof used the arrow), or it was to be redone already, and then runs the Shaky-like cascade from it, so what rests on it lists it in `because` and does not come back with the arrow either. *Alternative:* only a Result that was not to be redone yet, as before 2j1; the arrow's un-decline would then bring back a reverted Result whose question changed.
82. **D2-82** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): the cascade notice of a return is an event, like a cascade's "To be redone with it, …": "Back in review with it, since they depend on it:" for the items back in review and "Back to Shaky with it, since they depend on it:" for those back to declined Shaky, each with its linked items; the outcome's `cascade` lists them with "it depends on ‹label›". *Alternative:* one event for both, which would call a declined item "back in review".
83. **D2-83** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): D4's words. A record of kind `cascade` (or with no `kind`) shows one line per listed cause, "↻ to be redone: it depends on ‹label›, ‹state›", where the state is "declined Shaky", "rejected", "declined" (an arrow's plain decline), "to be redone", or else the cause's status word (an old record's cause back in review); with no listed cause, "↻ to be redone: telelean took it out: ‹reason›": "it depends on items that are shown with the results" when they are hidden, else its markers' reason, else "it depended on something that was declined" (builder 2j2, R1-1: past tense; it was "something it depends on was declined"). The other kinds show "telelean took it out: ‹reason›": `judge` "it no longer builds", "it no longer closes the gap", "something it depends on no longer builds or passes its checks" (a dependent) or "it no longer passes its checks"; `take-out` "another step of Submit review reworks what it depends on"; `queue` "it depends on items that are Shaky or to be redone"; `question` "“‹L›” left its question when you declined its arrow". The lines are `ItemView.redo.why`, built by `buildSessionView`. *Alternative:* send each cause's state and let the webview word it, which would put the words outside core.
84. **D2-84** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): one refusal text for every item to be redone, D2-76's own, which replaces `contentTakenOut`. *Alternative:* a second text for the kinds that do not come back by themselves (the judge, Submit's take-out, a queue's end, DO-0's question), where setting back what it depends on brings nothing back; the cause line says telelean took it out.
85. **D2-85** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): the session bar shows Submit's closed reason as text only in phase `stage`, before "Go to the first one"; while a step runs, the phase line already says why (D2-62), and Submit open shows none. *Alternative:* the text in every phase where Submit shows.
86. **D2-86** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): Submit's gate (view and runner) keeps a refusal for hidden results, with "The results are not shown yet: show them first.", though D2-76 makes it unreachable (every meaning item is listed, so a hidden stage always has an item in review, refused first). It reads real statuses only while the results are shown, fail closed. *Alternative:* drop the branch.
87. **D2-87** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1): a request's Accepts never take an item that the automatic return brought back in the same request; a group leaves it as not in review. The person sent the request while the item was to be redone, maybe with the results shown, and its return makes it undecided again; in two requests the second Accept is made with the results hidden (meaning before proof, the path AF4 guarded). No client sends such a request (one message per action, §5.1). *Alternative:* plan the Accepts on the state after the return, so a crafted request could accept a returned statement knowing the results.
88. **D2-88** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1, fixer round 1: F-F1 and A-ADV-1, major): an item the automatic return sends back to declined Shaky (a Shaky item a Reject cascade took out) does not count as "up" for the items to be redone because of it: a Shaky decline holds its dependents down (D2-48), so they stay to be redone, listing it in `because`, with their content back when the merge brings it (`reverted: false`); un-Shaky of it then brings them back. *Alternative:* count every returner as up (the first build), which put a dependent in review under a declined dependency until RV-3's next queue.
89. **D2-89** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1, fixer round 1: A-ADV-2, minor): an item the request un-declines into to be redone (a cascade casualty the person rejected, its cause un-rejected first) drops every id from `because` that holds up again (`dropHeldUp`): it stays to be redone, because its content is out of ws/ and only Claude brings it back (D2-78), and with no cause left it reads "telelean took it out: it depended on something that was declined" (builder 2j2, R1-1), not "it depends on ‹cause›, in review". *Alternative:* release it at once as a candidate; that needs its content in ws/, which a merge of its own Reject commit does not bring back.
90. **D2-90** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1, fixer round 1: F-F4, nit): the detail of a `judge` record that a declined cause joined (D2-80) names each declined cause first ("it depends on ‹label›, declined Shaky|rejected"), then the judge's reason. *Alternative:* the judge's reason alone (inaccurate for that cause).
91. **D2-91** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j1, fixer round 1: A-ADV-3, nit; not changed): the board's `proposedOpen` still draws an arrow item to be redone as Claude's proposal "still undecided": Claude proposes it again, so it is open on the board whatever the review counts. D1 changes the review's undecided-ness (§4.7), not that board drawing; no user decision asked. *Alternative:* draw it as decided, which would hide an arrow Claude is about to propose again.
92. **D2-92** USER DECISION (turn 25, 2026-11-22; built by 2j2): notes for Claude.
    - **N1. Every decline takes an optional note for Claude:** Reject, Shaky and the plain decline of arrow items (DO-0); Transform gets its note with the transform window (slice 4). A group decline shares one note, as Shaky's did. Core keeps `decline.note` for every option, a blank one dropped; the protocol allows `note` with `to: 'declined'` and any option (≤ `textLength`, may span lines). The decline menu shows "Note for Claude (optional)" for every option but the disabled Transform, an arrow item's single Decline and the group menus included; the detail shows the note of any declined item; Undo and Redo restore it with the decision.
    - **N2. An item to be redone takes a note too:** a new review action (`note`) sets, replaces or clears it, only while the item is to be redone and listed, in a stage, with the other decisions' refusals (a step running, a hidden item, outside a stage); it is a step on the session stack ("Note for Claude: ‹label›"), undone and redone, and persisted. It survives while the item stays to be redone (cascades that update `redo` keep it) and goes when the item leaves that status (2j1's automatic return, Undo). The footer of an item to be redone offers "Note for Claude…", edited in place, and shows the note.
    - **N3. One overall comment per Submit review:** when Submit will start Claude's steps, Submit review opens a small confirmation with what it does (`submitSteps`' words), "Comment for Claude (optional)", Submit and Cancel; when it leads to the end confirmation, no box and no extra click. `submitReview` carries an optional `comment` (same limit, may span lines, a blank one dropped), kept with the seal on the session stack and persisted; the next stage's report shows "Your comment at Submit review (stage n): …".
    - **N4. What Claude receives:** right after "Review submitted (stage n)", "The person's overall comment: “…”" (built with straight quotes, as SHAKY's note always was: D2-97); REJECTED and TO BE REDONE entries quote their notes as SHAKY's do ("  The person's note: “…”"), a declined arrow's where that arrow is listed; the decisions-since lines quote a decline's note.
    - **N5. Old sessions load unchanged:** additive optional fields only, no version bump.
    - **It supersedes** D2-13's "until slice 4, a Shaky decline takes an optional note" (its "Ask me instead" stays, and D2-13's alternative, one note at Submit, is now built beside it) and §2.4's "`note` only with `shaky`".
93. **D2-93** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j2): an item's note while it is to be redone is `ItemRecord.redo.note`. `copyRedo` copies it, and every rule that rebuilds `redo` while the item stays to be redone keeps it: a cascade that adds a cause, DO-0's question change, the judge's take-out of an item already to be redone, Submit's take-out, a return that leaves it to be redone. Whatever moves the record out of to be redone (a decline, an Accept, the automatic return, reconcile's new proposal or withdrawal; a declined record keeps `redo` when a cascade took its content out first, D2-60) drops it, by one sweep (`dropStaleRedoNotes`) at the end of every reducer action and of reconcile; Undo restores it with the recorded state. *Alternative:* a separate record field, cleared by each rule that changes a status.
94. **D2-94** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j2): the decline menu gives every option Shaky's flow: choosing Reject, Shaky or an arrow item's Decline shows "Note for Claude (optional)" and its confirm button ("Decline (Reject)", "Decline (Shaky)", "Decline"; Ctrl+Enter); R and S choose; an arrow-only menu (an arrow item's, or a group of arrow items) opens with its Decline chosen. The list's D key on an arrow item still declines at once, with no note (§5.5); the board's right-click "Decline…" always opens the menu, an arrow item's too (fixer 2j2 r1, F-F1). *Alternative:* one note field always shown, every option declining at once (one click fewer for Reject, but Shaky's and Ask me instead's flow would change).
95. **D2-95** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j2): the `note` action. Allowed only on a listed item to be redone: a hidden one is refused with "show them first" (D2-8), any other status with `noteOnlyRedo`; a stale rev is `conflict`. A blank note clears it; the same note again changes nothing and makes no stack step. It is one stack step, `note`, with the note before and after, labelled "Note for Claude: ‹label›", and a records-only write (no ws/ change, no judge). Undo needs the item still to be redone, at the same rev, with the note the step left (else `conflict`), and is refused while it is hidden; the view offers no Undo or Redo of a note step on an item hidden now (as for a decision, fixer 2c2 r1). Redo sends the note again through the same rules. *Alternative:* a decision `to: 'to-be-redone'` in `decideItems`, which would mix a field edit into the status rules.
96. **D2-96** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j2): Submit's comment is kept on its seal as `comment: { text, stage }` (the stage it submitted) and in its `queue-start` write (`comment`), so a replay after a crash seals it the same. The report shows the newest commented Submit seal whose stage is one below the current stage, or the current one when its queue failed and Retry (which keeps the stage) has since ended with no failure heading and only Retry's seals after it (fixer 2j2 r1, A-ADV-2j2-2); a later Submit without a comment never hides an older stage's: a Submit queue that failed leaves the stage as it was, so its comment (which Claude already received) is not shown as the next stage's. A comment sent with a Submit that leads to the end confirmation is dropped: no Claude step reads it, and no seal is made (D2-54). *Alternative:* a session field holding the comments by stage.
97. **D2-97** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j2): the decisions-since lines (§2.8) give each decide step its label, then each distinct note of its declines on its own line (`  The person's note: "…"`, straight quotes as SHAKY's note always was; fixer 2j2 r1, F-F5); a note step on an item to be redone gives its label and its note, or "  The person's note was cleared." The lines are the history of what the person did, so a note step on an item that has since left to be redone is still quoted, next to its "Back to review" line (fixer 2j2 r1, A-ADV-2j2-5: left as it is). *Alternative:* the labels alone, as before, so a mid-stage Formalize or Retry would never pass a note on.
98. **D2-98** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2j2): Submit's confirmation is an inline panel under the session bar's first line (no modal): `submitSteps`' sentence, "Comment for Claude (optional)" (≤ `textLength`, Ctrl+Enter submits), Submit and Cancel. It closes on Cancel, Esc, a Submit sent, and whenever the session no longer offers `claude-steps` (the gate closed, another phase). *Alternative:* a modal dialog.
99. **D2-99** AUTOPILOT DECISION (fixer 2j2 r1, F-F6, A-ADV-2j2-3, A-ADV-2j2-4): what the person typed is not lost to a refusal in the two surfaces 2j2 added. A refused Submit review (a stale stage, a conflict) puts its comment back in the panel while Submit still starts Claude's steps (`Pending.comment`). The note editor of an item to be redone stays open after Save until the host's view holds the note sent, so a refused note keeps its text and shows the error. The decline menu keeps its older behaviour (it closes when the decision is sent). "Ask me instead"'s prefilled sentence (telelean's, not the person's) is cleared when another option than Shaky is chosen while it is untouched (`DeclineMenuState.prefilled`). *Alternative:* leave all three as before; or restore the decline menu's note too.
100. **D2-100** USER DECISION (turn 27, 2026-11-23; built by 2k1): "I would like the Exit/Apply fix", as the orchestrator described it in turn 26, after the user pressed Exit session, confirmed, and the board kept showing the review panel unchanged (a second Exit then got "That run is not active any more.").
    - **D1. Once a session's run has ended, by any path** (Exit, Apply, Stop or Cancel, a failure, an end on recovery), the runner stops sending its review view: `session()` returns null for it, live and after a reload, and the change that ends the run tells the host that the session changed, so the host posts `session: null` to the board.
    - **D2. The board then shows the ended run's message with Dismiss,** exactly as for a non-session run: "You exited the edit session: your files were not changed…" after Exit; "Applied: the session's changes are in your files. One Undo takes the whole session back." after an Apply that changed files (the "Recorded" wording when it changed none, as `endedOutcome` already decides); the existing ended texts for Stop and failures. The session bar no longer offers Exit, Submit or any review control for an ended run; it shows what a non-session ended run shows.
    - **D3. Defence in depth on the board:** the webview never draws the review panel or the session bar's review controls for a run whose state is ended, even if a stale session view is still in its store or arrives late.
    - **D4. Dismiss** on an ended session run works as for any run (`manager.dismiss`) and clears the panel.
    - **D5. Nothing else changes:** Exit still changes no model file and records the attempts as discarded; Apply still writes one history entry and one Undo step; their confirmation modal and texts are unchanged; a second Exit sent in a race still gets the runner's refusal, but the board offers no Exit button for an ended run.
    - **D6. Tests:** §6.1's 2k1 list, with mutation checks of every key rule.
    - **D7.** This entry.
101. **D2-101** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k1): `session()` reads the run as last written (`run.saved`, as `current()` does): it is null from the run.json write that ends the run, the write whose change the host republishes on, so `current()` and `session()` never disagree about the end. The runtime's view stays (the end's write still rebuilds it), for `current()`'s question rule (AF5) and D2-10. *Alternative:* drop the runtime's view at the end, which would make `current()` read every ended session as one whose results are hidden (§3.12) and withhold an applied session's notices and activity; or read `run.ended`, which turns before that write lands, so the host could post the run still in review with no view ("Preparing the review…").
102. **D2-102** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k1): after the end, `current()` keeps D2-10 as it was. When the results were still hidden (an Exit before Show results, a Cancel while starting, a failure), the RunView withholds Claude's activity and every notice, the end's own included (run.json has it); the panel's line says how the session ended from the run's state (`REVIEW_TEXTS.exited`), so nothing on the board needs that notice. With the results shown (every Apply, an Exit after Show results), the end's notice is the RunView's last. *Alternative:* keep telelean's end notice in the RunView whatever the reveal; it tells nothing of the outcome, but the runner would have to tell it apart from a step's notices (by its text, or a new notice field), and the panel would show the line twice.
103. **D2-103** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k1): D2's "what a non-session ended run shows" covers the header too: the run badge and RunElsewhere of an ended session run read as any ended run's ("Claude: discarded · Spyro", "Claude’s run on Spyro: discarded."), not "Edit session: review · Spyro" or "An edit session is active on Spyro: the boards of this folder are read-only until it ends." *Alternative:* keep the session's words there, which would name a phase of a session that has ended and a lock that no longer holds.
104. **D2-104** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k1): D3's place is the store, not RunPanel's selector: `applyRun` clears the session view of a run that has ended (as `session: null` does: the review UI, the Proposed view, a session dialog), and `receive` reads a view that arrives for the run the store holds as ended as `session: null`. One rule then covers the review panel, the session bar, the Proposed overlay, the session's Undo scope and the session dialogs. A view of another run, or one that arrives with no run in the store, is kept as before. *Alternative:* RunPanel's `session` selector (and the session bar's and the badge's), which would leave a stale view in the store for the Proposed view, Undo's scope and the dialogs.
105. **D2-105** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k1): the Exit line is one text, core's `REVIEW_TEXTS.exited`, which the runner's notice and the board's line both use, as `REVIEW_TEXTS.applied` already was for Apply; `END_TEXTS.exited` goes. *Alternative:* a copy of the runner's text in the webview, two copies to keep equal.
106. **D2-106** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k1): one VS Code scenario, Exit (D6's minimum): an Apply scenario would have to decide every item, show the results, submit and wait for the final check through the board or the testing API, a long script that nobody can run before the integrator; Apply's ended outcome is covered by the runner's tests, the store's and Playwright's. *Alternative:* a second scenario for Apply.
107. **D2-107** USER DECISION (turn 27, 2026-11-23; built by 2k2): "make everything except the CLI's own conversation files and the fact extractor local to the project folder", as the orchestrator described it in turn 26, after the user deleted a model folder with an open session, made a new one at the same path, and found the old session's items, its consent, and on Exit its attempt record in the new folder (same `rootKey`).
    - **U1.** Everything telelean keeps for a model lives inside the model folder, except the Claude CLI's own conversation files (`~/.claude/projects/…`) and the shared Lean fact extractor. Deleting the folder deletes the rest; a folder recreated at the same path starts clean.
    - **U2.** Runs in `<model>/.telelean/runs/<runId>/`, today's layout inside; `/.telelean/runs/` in Initialize's `.gitignore` lines, and `.telelean/runs/.gitignore` with `*` when telelean creates the folder (built as `/*`, which git reads the same way: D2-118).
    - **U3.** Consent in the model folder, inside the git-ignored runs folder, asked once per folder with the same dialog.
    - **U4.** A moved, renamed or copied folder takes its runs along; a run recorded at another path cannot continue: only Exit (a session) or Stop (any other run), changing no model file.
    - **U5.** No migration: runs and consents in VS Code's storage are neither read nor deleted; the user guide says where they are.
    - **Requirements (orchestrator):** R1 git never sees run data; R2 run activity never looks like a model change (watcher, scans); R3 confinement holds with ws/ inside the model folder (the hidden-segment rule relative to ws/; the Lean sandbox never binds the model folder writable); R4 the CLI sees nothing more of the model folder (`GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`; CLAUDE.md and `.claude/`); R5 recovery and pruning per folder; R6 an inventory of other per-model state; R7 hidden results stay hidden from the board; R8 tests move to the new layout.
108. **D2-108** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2): the runs folder is derived from the root (`runsDirOf`), and `RunManagerOptions.runsDir` goes, so no caller can make a manager recover or prune elsewhere (R5); the run folder loses the `<rootKey>` level, which only told roots apart in a shared folder. `CreateShadowOptions.runsDir` stays (now the runs folder itself), so the shadow's own tests keep a scratch folder. *Alternative:* keep `runsDir` as an option that the host sets to `runsDirOf(root)`, leaving the rule to each caller.
109. **D2-109** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2): `ensureRunsDir` writes `.gitignore` (`/*`, D2-118) only when it is absent, at each run's creation and at the consent's write, so a folder made by an older build or by hand is covered; a `.gitignore` the user changed is left alone. *Alternative:* write it only when the folder is created, which would leave a folder created before 2k2 (or by hand) unignored.
110. **D2-110** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2): the consent file holds a small JSON record, and only its exact form counts; only a yes is written, as before (a no is asked again). *Alternative:* an empty marker file, which any stray file of that name would satisfy. (The record names the folder since the review's fix, D2-123.)
111. **D2-111** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2), U4: a run recorded at another path is held without its session layer, shown as an interrupted run with one Stop button and the notice, and its end records nothing in this folder (the safe default U4 names); a session's Exit is accepted too and does the same (its view shows nothing of Claude's work: D2-119). run.json stays untouched until the user ends it, so a folder moved back lets the run go on. **FOR THE USER:** the board offers that run "Stop", not "Exit session", because the review panel (where Exit lives) needs the session view, which would mean judging and replaying the run in a folder it did not start in; the Outcome's line is the plain panel's interrupted line, with the notice above it. *Alternative:* recover it through the session layer with the review panel, and refuse everything but Exit (a webview change, and judges and replays run for a run that cannot continue); or end it at once on recovery, which U4 does not ask for. (The same hold serves a live run whose own folder was deleted, D2-121.)
112. **D2-112** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2): pruning deletes a directory only when it is a run folder (a run-id name, and `run.json` or a shadow `git/` inside); files and other directories in `.telelean/runs/` are never deleted. *Alternative:* today's rule, which deleted any run-id-named directory whose run.json was missing, harmless in telelean's own storage but not in a folder the user can see and write.
113. **D2-113** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2): `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` is the run folder's real path, so git from ws/ (or below) looks only at ws/ itself (which never holds `.git`) and never at the run folder or above; it is set in `prepare()` on top of the sanitised environment, so the guard's self-test, the CLI and its hooks all get it; the sanitiser removes the host's `GIT_*` variables first (`STRIP_PREFIXES`), since a `GIT_DIR` or `GIT_WORK_TREE` from the host would point git at a repository without any search, past the ceiling. A run folder whose path holds a `:` (the ceiling's list separator) gets `GIT_DIR` = an absent directory in the run folder instead (resumed build). The CLI has no Bash tool; this is a second layer for anything it runs itself. *Alternative:* `GIT_DIR=/dev/null`, which would make every git call fail instead of finding nothing.
114. **D2-114** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2), **FOR THE USER to confirm:** no new guard against CLAUDE.md or `.claude/` in the directories above ws/. M0 established in the CLI binary that `--setting-sources=` loads no user, project or local settings and that `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_MDS=1` loads no CLAUDE.md (bundle 2's M0 report; `--setting-sources=` was pinned in `argvEnv.test.ts`, and the resumed 2k2 build pinned both there for a ws/ inside a model folder, the CLAUDE.md switch having had no test), and skills, commands and agents are out of reach (`--tools` lists only the five file tools; Agent is disallowed). The home directory's files were already above the old run folder. *Alternative:* the preflight refuses to start when a CLAUDE.md or `.claude/` exists between ws/ and the filesystem root (the home login folder excepted), which would refuse every model kept inside a project with its own CLAUDE.md.
115. **D2-115** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2): the board's in-place Lean build (`semantic/refresh.ts`) masks `<root>/.telelean/runs` with a tmpfs as it masks `.git`, so the model's own Lean code sees no run data, as before, when runs lived under the masked home. A run's own Lean jobs bind only that run's build folder, as before (no bind covers the model folder). *Alternative:* leave the runs folder readable to the board's build (read-only).
116. **D2-116** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2), R6: the board-restore records (workspaceState: each open board's title, root and subject) and the webview's panel state (VS Code's, per panel: subject, panel sizes, the review's current item for the same run id only) stay where VS Code keeps them: they describe the window, not the model, and name no run file. Test mode's `reset()` deletes `consent.json` of the roots it resets and of every folder this window wrote a consent into (D2-126), and no longer touches `globalState` (U5). History's refs stay in the repository that holds the folder (R6): for a model inside a larger repository they outlive the folder, so a folder recreated at the same place there finds the old History; the user guide says so. The telelean output channel's log (VS Code's logs folder, rotated by VS Code) names roots and run ids: diagnostics, not model data, kept. *Alternative:* keep them in the folder, which would make a window's layout travel with a copied model.
117. **D2-117** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2), **FOR THE USER:** the CLI's own deny rules (the second layer behind the path guard) are written only for a root path of `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` (`cliDenyRules`); ws/ now sits under the model folder, so a model folder whose path has a space or another character gets the guard alone, as the rule syntax for such paths was never verified (the CLI may not be run to check it). The guard, which the backend self-tests before every start, is unchanged. *Alternative:* refuse runs in such folders, or quote the paths in the rules without a check against the CLI.
118. **D2-118** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2, resumed build), **FOR THE USER:** `.telelean/runs/.gitignore` holds `/*`, not the `*` that U2's words name. Both make git ignore everything in the runs folder (each run's folder, `consent.json`, the file itself), so U2's purpose holds. But the Claude CLI's Grep runs ripgrep from ws/ (`<model>/.telelean/runs/<runId>/ws`) without `--no-ignore` (M0, bundle 2: `--hidden` and VCS exclusions only), and inside a git repository ripgrep applies the `.gitignore` files of the folders above its search root: a bare `*` there matches every file and folder of ws/, so every Grep of Claude's in a model with a git repository (Initialize makes one) would find nothing. `/*` is anchored to the runs folder and matches only its own entries, never a path below them; the model's `/.telelean/runs/` line, anchored to the model folder, does not reach below either. Not checked against the CLI itself (it may not be run); the unit tests pin the text, and `git status` ignores the folder with it (real git). *Alternative:* `*` as U2 says, with Claude's Grep finding nothing in such models.
119. **D2-119** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2, resumed build), R7: the view of a held run (D2-111) shows nothing of Claude's work, whatever its version: no activity, ghosts, freeze or proof verdict, and the moved-run notice as its only notice. A session's view already hid them (D2-10, no session view); a run of version 1 showed its own, and one left in review, which recovery in its own folder converts into a session with its results hidden (D2-16), would have shown them here. The masking holds while the run is held; once Stop has ended a held run of version 1, its view is its saved one again (practically unreachable: since 2k2 every run is version 2, and runs of older builds, in VS Code's storage, are never read, U5; D2-125). *Alternative:* the v1 run's view as saved, with the notice added.
120. **D2-120** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2): the two carry-overs of 2k1's review, which the chain gave the first 2k2 build as mandatory, are fixed in the webview (`model/run.ts`): the plain run panel drops a notice whose text equals the ended line it already shows (A-F1, `panelNotices`; run.json keeps the notice), and the run panel's session view is the board's run's own, on its subject, only while that run has not ended (F-F1, `panelSession`, a second layer behind D2-104). This reverses the 2k1 fixer's "not fixed, decided" for both (§8). *Alternative:* leave both as the 2k1 fixer decided (the duplicate line under "Earlier notices"; the store's D2-104 as the only layer).
121. **D2-121** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2, fixer round 1), the review's A-F1: a live run whose own folder is gone is held at once, as a moved one is (§2.10): the manager checks `<root>/.telelean/runs/<runId>/` where the run is read or acted on and at the drift timer; Claude's session and Lean jobs are stopped; Stop or Exit ends it writing nothing. This keeps U1's "nothing written into it from an old run" for the deletion under an open board, which the reload path (a new manager reads the new folder) never met. The registry's entry is not replaced when a folder at the same path is a new one (no identity check of the folder in `registry.get()`): the run, which is what held old data, is safe by itself, and replacing a workspace under an open board means closing and re-attaching that board, which cannot be tested here without VS Code. (How the old workspace's own in-memory model follows a replaced folder is not part of this change of where runs live.) *Alternative:* also compare the folder's identity (device and inode, or the birth time of `.telelean/config.json`) in `registry.get()` and `showBoard`, forgetting the entry and its board when it changed; or end such a run silently (drop it from the board) instead of showing it held.
122. **D2-122** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2, fixer round 1), **FOR THE USER to confirm,** the review's A-F6: each run folder holds a `.ignore` of `!*`, beside ws/ (written by `createRunShadow`). Inside the model's repository, ripgrep applies the model's own `.gitignore` (and the user's global ignore and `.git/info/exclude`) to paths below ws/, so a model rule such as `Model/` would hide those files from Claude's Grep, which Read and Glob still see; before 2k2 ws/ lay outside any repository. The reviewer measured it with ripgrep 15.0.0: `.ignore` with `!*` brings the files back. Not checked by this fixer (no ripgrep on this machine) nor against the CLI's own ripgrep (the CLI may not be run); the unit test pins the text. *Alternative:* state the limitation in D2-118 and the user guide, and leave Grep blind to what the model's `.gitignore` names.
123. **D2-123** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2, fixer round 1), **FOR THE USER to confirm,** the reviews' F-F4 and A-F3: the consent record names the model folder (`"root"`, its real path, else its resolved path), and only the exact text for this folder counts. U3 says a copied or cloned model never carries the consent: git-ignoring keeps it out of a clone, but a file-manager copy (`cp -r`) carries the whole folder, `consent.json` included, so the copy would have run Claude without asking. Now a copy, a move or a rename asks again (it asked again before 2k2 too, the consent being keyed by the folder's path); a symlinked spelling of the same folder does not. *Alternative:* keep the record without the folder and say "a clone" in D2-110, §2.10 and the guides (a plain copy would then carry the consent).
124. **D2-124** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2, fixer round 1), the reviews' F-F8 and A-F4: the moved-run test (D2-111) compares run.json's root with the manager's root by path, and, when they differ, by real path, so a model reached through a symlink (a symlinked `~/telelean-demos` is plausible) once and through its real path another time is not held as moved, which would have forced the user to Stop a live session. A root that cannot be resolved any more counts as another folder. *Alternative:* the lexical comparison alone.
125. **D2-125** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2, fixer round 1), the reviews' F-F9 and A-F7: D2-119's masking is stated to hold while the run is held; the ended view of a held run of version 1 is its saved one. Practically unreachable (every run of 2k2 is version 2; older runs are in VS Code's storage and never read, U5), so no code. *Alternative:* mask the ended view as the held one (drop activity, ghosts, proof verdict and the notices but the end's).
126. **D2-126** AUTOPILOT DECISION (2k2, fixer round 1), the review's F-F10: the host remembers every folder it wrote a consent into, and Test mode's `reset()` forgets those besides the roots it resets, so a consent in a folder whose workspace was forgotten earlier (`disposeWorkspace`) does not survive a reset and make the VS Code suites order-dependent. *Alternative:* accept it, as the reviewer allowed.

### 7.3 Carried in from slice 1 (for the integrator)
- **Leaks the designs found in slice 1's code.** The review-UI and checks designs found that the 1d Proposed view shows the outcome before the reveal:
  1. downstream cards get the "changed" badge and the "result after the review" chip only when the target was proved, and the banner's changed count rises;
  2. the hidden target's expanded card shows `lean.code`, which includes the new proof;
  3. new proof-only lemmas show in the Vocabulary band ("+N new") and in Used by;
  4. Raw Lean diff… opens without counting as Show result.

  The session-engine design found a fifth, since M2:

  5. attempts.jsonl gets the round's verdict at round end, and the Current board's card details list it.

  Slice 2 removes all five (§4.7, §5.10, D2-11).
- **For the slice-1 `dist` build** (AP-8), the integrator decides whether 1g first fixes leaks 1–4 in the webview. The review-UI design proposes a small fix:
  - the hidden set = the target plus the cards downstream of it in the current graph, shown with base statuses;
  - DiffBadge and the counts ignore the proof and status fields of hidden cards;
  - CardDetails shows the current `.holds` text for a hidden target;
  - the proposal's lemmas stay out of "+N new" until revealed;
  - Raw Lean diff asks first, then reveals.

  Recommended: it is webview-only and small.
- **Recorded facts:** regeneration is still pending from 1d, and is done together with `typeConsts`, with the isolated probe, at the end of 2b3a (§2.1, D2-75).
- **FakeBackend fixtures:** their first line `Run: "These suffice"` stays. Renaming it goes with those fixtures (the integrator's call, outside slice 2).

### 7.4 Patches and logs
- After each sub-slice, the integrator saves `verbatim_prompt_log/bundle_21/evidence/patches/after_slice_2x.patch`: every file changed or created since the baseline, excluding `package-lock.json`.
- The builder's report goes in `bundle_21/subagent_reports/`.
- At the slice end:
  - the integrator logs §7.2's decisions in `02_autopilot_decisions.md` (one AP entry, listing D2-1–D2-75, the ones withdrawn by AP-24 marked as such, and how to reverse each). The entry also warns that reversing slice 2 (`git apply -R` of its patches) leaves any open session unrecoverable, since the older build reads only version-1 run.json: Exit open sessions first. It also says, in plain words, that the Lean check's probe now runs as its own job (D2-75), so AP-21's "the check runs again without the probe" is gone, and a model whose names clash with the probe's imports now keeps its constants; that a model whose probe fails (too large, or an error of the walk on its own files) can no longer start a Formalize, with the cause named before anything is sent to Claude; and that Claude may no longer write the `init` attribute, which ran Claude's code in every Lean job that imported it (AP30-1, AP30-2). It adds that this protection assumes the model's files import nothing but their own modules: if the person's files ever import Lean's own modules (`import Lean`), Claude's files get other ways to run code in those jobs, which telelean does not refuse (AP30-7);
  - then a "slice 2 done" entry.
